> AGENT

Done. Here's what changed:

- **`e2e.yml`**: Removed `pull_request` trigger and the `changes` detection job (no longer needed since it only runs on push to main / manual dispatch)
- **Branch protection**: Removed `E2E Tests` from required status checks

PR CI is now just: lint, typecheck, unit tests, API spec check, cargo checks, and PR title lint. The heavier stuff (web E2E, desktop builds, desktop E2E) all run after merge to main.

> AGENT

Now I see the full picture. The build.rs looks for `fuse` then `osxfuse` via pkg-config, but FUSE-T installs `fuse-t.pc` at `/Library/Application Support/fuse-t/pkgconfig/`. On my local machine I also have macFUSE which provides `fuse.pc` at `/usr/local/lib/pkgconfig/` — that's why it works locally.

The fix is to add a step that creates a `fuse.pc` symlink (or copies the `fuse-t.pc` as `fuse.pc`) so pkg-config can find it, OR add `/Library/Application Support/fuse-t/pkgconfig` to the search path and update the build.rs to also probe `fuse-t`.

The cleanest approach: create a symlink so the existing build.rs works without modification.

> AGENT

PR created: https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/225

**Root cause**: FUSE-T's pkg installer places its pkgconfig file at `/Library/Application Support/fuse-t/pkgconfig/fuse-t.pc` (named `fuse-t`, not `fuse`). Our vendored fuser `build.rs` probes for `fuse` or `osxfuse`. On my local machine it worked because macFUSE was also installed (providing `fuse.pc`), but CI only has FUSE-T.

**Fix**: After installing FUSE-T, symlink `fuse-t.pc` → `/usr/local/lib/pkgconfig/fuse.pc`. Applied to all 4 workflow files that install FUSE-T.

> AGENT

Found it. `github_owned_allowed: true` covers `actions/*`, and `verified_allowed: true` covers verified creators. But `GabrielBB/xvfb-action@v1` and `ikalnytskyi/action-setup-postgres@v8` aren't covered by either — they need to be in the `patterns_allowed` list.

> TOOL

tool_use Bash

  "description": "Add missing actions to allowed list"
}

> AGENT

The `Find latest CI run ID` step has no `shell:` specified, so Windows uses its default (PowerShell). It needs `shell: bash`. But there's a bigger issue — this is the `workflow_dispatch` path. When triggered via `workflow_run` (the normal flow), this step is skipped and `github.event.workflow_run.id` is used instead.

The real problem for the `workflow_run` trigger is that the CI run on main didn't produce desktop artifacts because the desktop build jobs are now gated to `github.event_name == 'push' && needs.changes.outputs.desktop == 'true'` — and the changes detection may not have flagged desktop changes.

But let me focus on what we can fix. Two issues:

1. The `find-run` step needs `shell: bash` for cross-platform compat
2. The `workflow_run` path — the CI that triggered this didn't build desktop artifacts because the desktop build is now conditional

> TOOL

> AGENT

Both recent CI runs on main failed (PR #225 and #223). The symlink fix PR #225 also failed — likely because it was merged before the symlink fix could take effect on itself. Neither run produced desktop artifacts.

There are actually two separate issues to fix:

1. **`find-run` step needs `shell: bash`** — PowerShell can't parse bash syntax
2. **The `download-artifact` step will fail** when `workflow_run` triggers because the CI run that triggered it had no desktop build artifacts (desktop builds failed or were skipped)

Let me fix both:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01V8d2t6jJxtvhKMDddFzi6M
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/michael/Code/cipher-box/.github/workflows/e2e-desktop.yml",
  "old_string": "      - name: Find latest CI run ID\n        if: github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch' && inputs.run_id == ''\n        id: find-run\n        run: |\n          RUN_ID=$(gh run list --workflow=ci.yml --branch=main --status=success --limit=1 --json databaseId --jq '.[0].databaseId')\n          if [ -z \"$RUN_ID\" ] || [ \"$RUN_ID\" = \"null\" ]; then\n            echo \"No successful CI run found on main branch.\" >&2\n            exit 1\n          fi\n          echo \"run_id=$RUN_ID\" >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\n        env:\n          GH_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}",
  "new_string": "      - name: Find latest CI run ID\n        if: github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch' && inputs.run_id == ''\n        id: find-run\n        shell: bash\n        run: |\n          RUN_ID=$(gh run list --workflow=ci.yml --branch=main --status=success --limit=1 --json databaseId --jq '.[0].databaseId')\n          if [ -z \"$RUN_ID\" ] || [ \"$RUN_ID\" = \"null\" ]; then\n            echo \"No successful CI run found on main branch.\" >&2\n            exit 1\n          fi\n          echo \"run_id=$RUN_ID\" >> \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\n        env:\n          GH_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}"
}
```

> AGENT

Here's the situation:

1. **Fixed**: `find-run` step now has `shell: bash` so it works on Windows
2. **Fixed** (in PR #225, already merged): FUSE-T pkgconfig symlink so macOS builds can find `fuse.pc`
3. **Underlying issue**: The e2e-desktop workflow can't run yet because the last two CI runs on main both failed — no desktop artifacts were produced. Once the current PR merges and CI passes on main with desktop changes, the e2e-desktop workflow will trigger and find the artifacts.

The e2e-desktop workflow will work once a CI run on main succeeds with the desktop build jobs (which requires desktop file changes to trigger them, per the gating condition). You may want to either:
- Push a trivial desktop change to main to trigger the desktop builds, or
- Temporarily make the desktop build condition less strict (e.g. always build on push to main regardless of path changes)
